Artificer's Crown Lore":"While the shoulderpieces are a tribute to a craftman's diligence and hard work, the Crown is passed on from person to person as a symbol of achievement. It's awarded for the most exquisite work of art annually, and the owner of the Crown is granted discounts and lucrative deals for any materials and resources they need.","Grasp Of The Grave Lore":"An ancient tradition among the Necromancers encourages them to use the bones of their teachers and masters to imbue their equipment with necrotic power. The legendary Grasp Of The Grave is the most famous and mythical example, covering users' hand and wrist with a skeletal hand, each singular bone gathered from a different and powerful Necromancer of old. Each owner passes it on to his apprentice, tasking him with adding a bone of his own after his death. The hand is almost complete nowadays, and legend says the Necromancers will come to power once the last bone is in place.","Legacy Lore":"These boots were worn by the first explorer of the Void (at least the first one to come back). Just an ordinary pair of traveling boots during that time, decades-long exposure to the enigmatic Void energies made them one of the most coveted accessory of the Voidmancers. The energy they radiate is so great that they practically create a local, permanent portal to the Void, which endlessly seeps onto the ground.","Light Of Eighth Star Lore":"While most people of the Realm aknowledge only the pantheon of Six, with the Church of the Seventh Star worshipping their own, Seventh Deity, there's a tale that's told by commonfolk and narrated to children - the unseen, benevolent Eighth Star. The story goes, in the beginning the pantheon counted 8 Stars-Deities, who worked in unison and harmony. Then the Six gods of primal forces began a rivalry, comparing their might and influence, sowing destruction on mortal worlds as they did. The Seventh Star declared others inferior and demanded others bow before him, but was cast down by the combined strength of the Six. The Eighth Star, a tender soul, tried to stop the conflict, but all his efforts were in vain. Disappointed in his fellow gods, he went into exile. To this day, once a year, he returns and tries to reason the other Six to make peace. While he never succeeds, the days when the Eighth is back are the days of peace and prosperity for the mortal Realms, as the Gods cease their warmongering and try to reason with each other. This rare necklace can sometimes be found in most seclude and serene places of the land, powered by unknown force and bolstering the wearer's creative potential - as if the Eighth is blessing the owner to create, instead of destroying.","Miniaturized Accelerator Lore":"This intricate necklace is the result of complex effort to make a common equipment item mimic the actions of a seasoned spellcaster, which is usually accompanied by some sort of a focus. Sadly, the only plausible success was achieved with the Accelerator. Nevertheless, the research is still ongoing...","Collar Of Obedience Lore":"This large collar belonged to the first Spellhound that mutated into an Arcanaworg, to ensure her obedience in case the mutation would lead to instability. Later, once the owner was certain that no harm came to the hound's mind, the collar was taken off and the creature went for the throat the very second it was unbuckled, killed her owner and ran away, never to be seen again. Later, houndmasters came to the conclusion that the Worg became intelligent enough to realise the owner didn't trust her and became embitterred by this over time. The artifact itself absorbed some of the mutation's energy and became a potent source of power for a wide range of Pets. Its owners now use it as a piece of clothing - but wear it on the waist, both for the sake of not wearing a collar on the neck, and out of fear it could still instill obedience like it did in the past.","Architectural Databelt Lore":"This belt eases the incredibly arduous task of actually finding a place for new sources of mana and managing the infrastructure, which arose as the usage of Red Catalysation became popular.","The Amplifier Lore":"The benefits of spells, utilizing accumulative power, are known to almost every wizard, but the most renowned users of such spells are the Ironsoul warriors of the east. One of their ancient tradition included honoring the one warrior, achieving the most tranquil state among them. The winner's prize was a large metal torq, meant to be worn around one's shoulders. Following the tumultuous events of recent years, the tradition was slowly abandoned, and the torq ended up in hands of Ishivar, an eastern scholar. After a series of experiments, he realized the torq somehow absorbed the essence, or, rather, the sheer fact, of a spell being cast, recording it. Adorning it with an magic-repelling cloth, which reflected the absorbed energy back to the caster, he created the Amplifier. The Amplifier practically doubles each spell that benefits from accumulating effects, 'tricking' the magical laws.","The Magnifier Lore":"The Amplifier's potential was researched and expanded upon in more recent times. Based on the fact that Persistent spells were developed upon the concept of Accumulated spells by the scholars, it was theorized that, with some modifications, it could affect those, instead. The result came to be known as \"the Magnifier\"","The Bond Lore":"The connection between a Wizard and his Pet has always been a unique source of power both for an apprentice just starting out and a mighty spellslinger dabbling deep into the arcane. There is a legend about a certain sorcerer and his Pet, who lived ages ago. From the first days of magic school and unto their later years, the two always stayed together. The magician made sure to tend to his beloved creature, feed, raise and educate it, and the creature was always at his side, sharing the joys of victories and comforting in times of despair. But, as years passed, there came a time when the two had a falling out. The wizard grown in power so much he felt no need for the Pet anymore, and the Pet was sad and angry over feeling unneeded. After all, the wizard's path would've been impossible without it. So it challenged the wizard, to decide once and for all, who's more important - the Wizard, or the Pet. The battle raged, and to the mage's surprise, he couldn't get the upper hand without support. It's said that, in the end, both became ash, as no one yielded. According to the myth, this ring is the only thing that found where the battle took place.","Whiplash Lore":"Many wizards treat their Pets as allies, friends or even equals - yet there's a group of spellcasters that believe absolute obedience and tireless work is paramount. There's a certain truth to their beliefs - often, Pets don't really work at their maximum efficiency, since they're not hard-pressed enough to do so. This ring was crafted with this one purpose in mind - it makes the owner look much more strong, brutal and demanding to his Pet, inspiring a mix of fear, loyalty and awe in the creatures at his command. The results prove to be quite spectacular, causing each Pet to work much faster and harder.","Habitstone Lore":"A stone older than most schools of wizardry, the Habitstone looks like a simple, pink rock with a chipped side. A closer look reveals myriads of infinitely-thin layers of colors on the inside. The story tells of a primitive sorcerer, who mistakenly thought the stone (a tiny, round pebble at the time) was the source of his power. He cast many a spell over the course of his life, and his 'pupil' inherited the stone. In time, his descendants realized the stone was a simple trinket - but by the time this was discovered so much magic was used around the stone, it became infused with arcane power. The stone is said to be pretty capricious - granting his owner power only once he's added a lot of new layers upon it.","Commissar's Torn Sleeve Lore":"Once exclusively coveted by the church of the Seventh Star, the Comissar's Coat was a renowned relic, tradionally worn by highest-ranking officers during large-scale conflicts. At some point in time, the coat itself was lost, along with it's latest owner. The investigation following his disappearance found only the coat's sleeve, and some blood, right inside his living chambers.","Temporal Scabbard Lore":"A compact, but complex device that applies a localized temporal distortion onto the wearer's weapon. A small note on the backside reads: Manufacturer takes no responsibility in case of scabbard not working on your particular choice of weapon.","Zenith, The Champion's Steed Lore":"This enigmatic horse seems to be present across timelines, Realms and realities for some obscure reason - choosing to serve a true champion among the wizards every century.","Warp Basilisk Lore":"A valuable beast, found during an expedition to yet uncharted regions. It seems to be able to blink in and out of the void at will, almost teleporting due to time anomalies found there. This enabled a skilled rider to maneuver quickly and avoid any positioning issues.","The Rubedo Engine Lore":"In rare events and timelines where true prowess of Alchemy and Magic cross and exchange experience, amazing discoveries are often made. While this machine is a convenient transportation method, it's primary purpose is to accumulate spellcasting power and techniques to practically no limit.","Nulldragon, The Anomaly Lore":"Same as Void creatures were discovered by the first explorers of the Void, the fabric of existence, the place where Gods, logic and laws of nature manifest, was also found to house smaller entities. Like a random fluctuation of math, an unmeasurable value, this serpent flows through reality, unseen and feeds upon remnants of broken universal laws.","Ritual Disk Lore":"A prized floating disc awarded to the most promising young wizards, it allows the user to store magical augmentations upon its surface, reducing the burden of the spellcaster.","Broomstaff Of Klevdariah Lore":"Klevdariah was a Necromancer apprentice, raised as a servant, in a society tied to traditions of utilizing the dead and their souls for sinister purposes. While the world around her got twisted by the cataclysmic changes brought about, she came of age and one day got lost in the woods, coming in contact with the Druids of a neighbouring marsh. Despite her worst fears, she was not persecuted nor killed, and instead was taught about the cycle of life and the value of balance. Friendships, joy and cheerful memories of those times have changed her forever. She's now a keeper of balance, playing pranks and watching over the natural cycle and the denizens of the lands under her watch. Common people both revere her, and fear her wrath, for she'd not hesitate to bring plague, rot and death upon the wicked. Her iconic Broomstick, doubling as her spellcasting staff, is only accompanied by her laughter on a full moon.","Crystallized Echo Lore":"Over time, Echoes accumulating across endless Realms gradually left an enigmatic form of residue upon the Traps that magic-users utilize to contain them. This residue crystallized and grew, until it started to become its own form of Void being. These beings are naturally attuned to help wizards collect and accumulate more Echoes than their normal Traps would allow.","Shadow-Scryer's Crystal Ball Lore":"Many crystal balls are simple glass, novelties for the masses or raw material for practitioners of the arts to weave their own enchantments upon. However, when an Ebonsand Behemoth's master falls in battle, a few grains of sand that once made it up are imbued with a portion of its essence - and the last vengeful commandments of its fallen master. Crystal balls made from this sand have a shred of sentience of their own and tend to show those foolhardy enough to gaze into them gruesome visions of their own demise. Most mages have better things to do than argue with the capricious spirits that occupy these orbs. However, those who call themselves shadow-scryers prize such rarities. Scryers spend months at a time learning each new orb's peculiarities until they can tame it and enlist its aid. The orbs can aid spellcasting, demonstrating a myriad of forgotten techniques to hasten spellcasting, saving a heartbeat here and a syllable there. Those who gaze long enough find themselves weaving whole alphabets of arcane language effortlessly as one spell compounds upon the next.","Head Of The All-Eater Lore":"The first Hungerer ever brought to material plane proved to be quite a menace. Its owner thought it harmless when starved of magical energy, but he couldn't be more wrong. While he was asleep, the creature fed upon everything else it found in the town. Next morning, the owner woke up only to find himself upon his bed, amidst an empty crater, with his pet sleeping peacefully by his side - the Hungerer didn't harm him, clearly still loyal to its master. In the end, with heavy heart, he had to fight and kill it before it could cause any more destruction. But due to the creature's immense power and infusion by the Void, its skull seems to have some semblance of life on its own - still consuming whatever is fed to it. Having no body, though, it gives it back in form of pure energy. But you, my dear thread, have done something I have not been able to achieve - even though I have been blessed with the gift of possibility.'\n- 'Nice to hear I've at least done something with that bold move. But what about the world?'\n- 'Forget the world. It is but a single point in an infinite line. But you... What are you? How could you achieve it? It feels like... Meeting a long forgotten nemesis.'\n- 'I'd bet you're some sort of divine being or something. So I'm not sure what you're talking about. But sure! Tell me more then! We do have the time, I guess? If anything ever made me go on, it's curiousity. Seems like I don't have anything else left, anyway.'\n- 'Hahaha, oh how familiar! How peculiar! Very well then. Watch me.'\n\nKelphior felt drawn into some abstract space, a plane between planes. Masses of essences, not unlike him, formed around and slowly conglomerated into something singular. Suddenly he realized this sensation of reality, a connection he'd always felt yet never understood - just as he felt it gently slip away. As if some unseen, ghastly hand has swept through the entire world, separating what it deemed necessary from the whole. Another powerful presence came forward. Its grasp was enormous, bigger than any words can describe. Bigger than stars, bigger than... Everything? It grabbed the world in its entirety, and squeezed it. Denser and smaller it grew, until everything disappeared in a blinding flash. Then, it stretched anew, and a tremendous force roared throughout the cosmos, signaling a new beginning. A new world was born. A new Realm. \n\n- 'For anyone else, I'd say you'll be soon entering this new world. But... You're not new, are you. Something like you will, perhaps, always be. Something that's akin to what you were. A clueless mewling in the darkness of world's mysteries. But what you are now...'\n- 'What _am_ I now? Not exactly human, I gather?' He felt both surprised and intrigued.\n\nThe figure paused once again.\n\n- 'You are our prodigy, \nour newest kinsman\nOur eyes of reason; \nArise, Cerebros. The Eighth Reborn.'\n\nWith his knowledge now growing infinitely, he felt no guilt. He knew that his act of defiance destroyed the whole world he lived in. In an instant, he killed every single creature he knew, in every timeline that ever existed. But that was necessary to create a hope of a world not bound by the inescapable doom, which he tried to prevent for so long, all in vain. Those lives, compared to the ones created in infinite other worlds, are nothing. Their loss is nothing. A simple, rational decision. It will look like this and appear at the bottom of the screen.","Spells Text 3":"Spell Scrolls are where you place your Spells. Clicking a Spell Scroll will open up the Spellbook, which contains all of the Spells available to your current Character Class. Most Spells also require a minimum Character level to be used.\n\nSome Spells are shared between one or more Classes, while some are exclusive to a certain one.\n\nTo cast a Spell you must first place it in the Spell Scroll. Spells removed from the panel do not provide any benefit, with a few rare exceptions. Once it is placed, it starts charging (the blue bar fills up) by consuming Spell Shards. Once it consumes enough, it gains a Charge and can be cast. Refer to Spell Shards section for more information on sources of Shards and their mechanics.\n\nTake note that casting a spell only yields a \"Times used\" after its duration expires, if any.\n\nAll Spells belong to one of three categories: Evocation, Incantation and Summoning, which are shown below.","Spells Text 4":"Spell types come in play in various situations you'll encounter later in the game, but generally an Evocation provides some instant profit, Incantation grants a bonus for a certain time, and Summoning summons a minion that performs autoclicks for you.\n\nThere are also three distinct sub-categories of Spells, which can fall into any of the three categories above: Accumulated, Persistent and Augment Spells. Accumulated Spells increase the bonus they provide with the amount of times this Spell was cast this Exile. Persistent Spells consist of an Active and a Passive component. Active component acts as a normal Spell, while the Passive one grants its bonus whenever the Spell is selected into one of Scrolls - regardless of being active or not. The Passive component slowly grows in power with amount of casts this Spell has across <i>all</i> Exiles, allowing you to carry it over. Augment spells are always providing their bonus even when they're not put onto the spellbar and usually get stronger with the amount of their casts performed.","Spells Shards Text 1":"This icon represents Spell Shards.","Spells Shards Text 2":"These are required to cast Spells. Most Spells require a specific amount of Shard to gain a Charge, which you can then spend to cast the Spell. Some special Spells are called Charge-based or Charging Spells and usually use some specific mechanic to acquire Charges instead.\n\nSources of Spell Shards include: Passive gain granted every second, Autoclicks that grant 1/5 of your Shards per Click per Autoclick performed and some specific Spells and Pets mechanics.\n\nAll Shards gained are accumulated into the Shard Pool and then 10% of your total is distributed into your selected spells every 0.1 second. Notable exceptions are Lightning Bolt and Spell Focus spells, which feed into other Spells on the panel directly, instead. The Pool has an upgradeable cap of Shards it can accumulate.","Catalysts Text 1":"Catalysts are a special type of resource associated with Mana Sources.\nThey come in three varieties: Green, Red and Blue.","Catalysts Text 2":"These are not obtained directly. You can earn Catalyst Shards via various activities, namely Expedition and Trials.\n\nCatalysts are used to improve your Mana Sources. The price of improving grows exponentially, with green being the cheapest and blue being the most expensive.\n\nWhen you earn Shards, a random Source is selected for \"catalyzation\", and gives you a number of Shards equal to its current Catalyst level of corresponding color multiplied by various bonuses to Catalysts earned.\n\nDo note that Source amount granted by Red Catalysts do not count towards challenge requirements, unlock requirements, achievemets and other source amount checks. Both serve as a goal and an indicator of your overall progress.\n\nParagons are based off your current Mysteries obtained. They are paramount to your progress. Paragon levels unlock various ingame mechanics. Once you reach a certain Mysteries level, it's unlocked automatically - which usually occurs upon Exiling.\nParagons do not reset upon Exiling, but DO reset upon switching Realms.\n\nLegacy levels are even more global and persistent through both Exiling and Realm switches. They provide more subtle bonuses and are unlocked via achieving various goals, such as Catalyst shards obtained, max Character level reached, and more.","Journal Text 3":"The second tab deals with the Challenges. These are a separate game mode. It's similar to Exiling, and starting one requires you to Exile. Once you do, you start a new run with a fixed set of rules and goal. Various mechanics can be either disabled, or not count for the purpose of global stats. Completing a Challenge often rewards you with some unique bonus.\n\nIt should be noted that while switching Realm locks you out of Challenge rewards, the fact of completion remains - which means that as soon as you meet the Challenge's requirements you immediately receive its bonus without the need to complete it a second time.","Journal Text 4":"The third tab deals with Trials. All Trials require you to complete a certain activity to receive a reward, and are activated by spending a Rune.\n\nEach Trial has both a loot pool and an additional reward. The additional one is guaranteed upon completion, while the primary one bestows one of the pool at random. You can inspect what each of Trials needs you to do by mousing over it in the Trials tab.\n\nThe fourth tab contains all the Lore fragments, these are special messages or short stories you uncover as you progress through the game.\n\nThe fifth tab contains Quests. Quests are designed to teach you specific mechanics that are integral to your success in the game.","Attributes Text 1":"Attributes are a system of points you can distribute to receive certain bonuses.\n\nAmount of points you have depends on two factors:\n1) Bonus points received via Challenge rewards.\n2) Points acquired over time. These are accumulated passively, and the time required to receive the next one depends on a factor of Mysteries you have and your current Character level.\n\nEach attribute type gives you a certain bonus for each point invested. The bonus is <i>multiplicative</i>, so investing multiple points does not suffer from any diminishing returns.\nIn addition to that, each attribute has a set of milestones, requiring a multiple of 25 points invested to achieve. These milestones can vary from utility to some incredibly powerful bonuses, and one should always strive to unlock those, if possible.\n\nAnother important role of attributes is unlocking Items and Pets. Most Items require a minimum amount of attribute points invested to use, and many Pets also require some. \n\nYou can also attain the minimum requirement by equipping certain Items that bestow bonus attributes. While Items won't be usable if you remove the Item, the Pets will persist after being selected.","Expedition Text 1":"Expedition is a separate (mini)game mode, that can be reached by using the following button.","Expedition Text 2":"Expedition is a game mode where you find and combat various monsters by sending your Apprentices on Expeditions. The Expedition is comprised of a number of Locations which your Apprentices can gradually unlock by completing the previous ones. Once you begn it, the Apprentices will gradually complete it encounter after encounter on their own.\n\nExpedition features a separate level progression - the levels affect the chance of your success in Expeditions. Once you reach the maximum level, you will instead begin to receive Consumables and Key Fragments every time you accumulate enough Experience.\n\nExpedition Consumables are comprised of several items you can use to improve your ability to complete Expeditions. These can be found by defeating monsters or obtained in the game's Market.\n\nDefeating an Enemy grants you Expedition XP, Catalyst Shards and Trophy items for the main game mode. After a Trophy is of Legendary quality, new drops will now grant you Exhibits - a special upgrades to the Trophy, increasing its values.\n\nIt should be noted that Expedition persists through Exiles and Realm switches in full, retaining the Level, progression and Consumables.\n\nAside from normal, open locations, there are Key-locked locations. To make an attempt at completing it, you'll need to spend a certain amount of Keys. These Keys can be obtained from the Experiments, as an additional reward from the Talent Trial and from Bats. Be aware that the entry fee is a single completion, and you'll be required to pay the fee to enter again.\n\nThere are also daily special bonuses to a random set of locations, granting extra loot or rewards but also increasing the Location's level.","Items Text 1":"Items are the gear you can equip to receive various powerful bonuses.\nThese are some examples of Items found in the game.","Items Text 2":"You can access the item screen via the Character screen (accessed by clicking the Portait in the top part of the screen, or the bottom-left corner button) by clicking the Equipment sub-tab there.\n\nThere, you can Craft items. By investing some dust, you move the Crafting progress bar, and receive an item when it reaches 100%. The item you receive is chosen at random from the Item slots you currently have available.\n\nRefer to \"Crafting Dust\" for more information on Dust.","Items Text 3":"Items persist through Exiles and Realms.\n\nItems are not generated at random, and you cannot destroy them or receive a duplicate. You can also spend your dust on Experiments (unlocked at e200 Mysteries), instead, to receive various rewards.\n\nWhen you unlock Crafting, you possess 4 Craftable slots, and unlock some other ones further in the game.\n\nItems can be of 6 quality levels - Common, Uncommon, Rare, Epic, Legendary and Unique. All items begin at a set quality level, and can be further improved by investing some dust.","Items Text 4":"Unique items are crafted at Unique quality from the start and cannot be upgraded.\n\nSome items form Item Sets, and bestow additional bonuses when worn together (granted they all meet Attribute requirements).\n\nYou can access Item Loadouts by using the following button.","Items Text 5":"This allows you to remember a loadout of equipped items and switch them via hotkeys as necessary. Every class possesses a unique set of loadouts - you can change them freely, without affecting other classes' item sets.","Items Text 6":"At e1050 Mysteries you unlock the ability to create Mythic items.\n\nOnly one Mythic item can be equipped at any time (but you can possess up to 30). These items have a base \"inherent\" bonus that depends on the item type and 3 random bonuses. The Enchant type is freely changeable at any time, with the pool of available Enchant types matching the Enchant types that can be found on regular items in this slot. The random bonuses can be rerolled for Nullifiers.\n\nThese items can be duplicated and you can possess any amount of any basic type (up to the total cap).\n\nLater on, you obtain the ability to add Imbuements to these items and Masterwork them. Imbuing is a Mythic mechanic which allows you to expand the functionality of your Mythic items by adding up to 2 new powers (Imbuements) to them. First, you select a specific pool of Imbuements to choose from, then the resulting power is selected from that pool at random. These Imbuements are not affected by Proficiency. Masterworking is yet another way to upgrade Mythic items that increases all Inherent bonuses, rolled bonuses and Imbuements.","Items Text 7":"At e1300 Mysteries you also unlock Proficiency. It's a mechanic that allows you to make your existing regular Items stronger. Proficiency Shards can be earned through an Experiment that unlocks at the same time. Instead of investing into Items themselves, Proficiency Shards are instead invested into an Item Slot, making any item used in that slot stronger. It increases its basic bonuses by a certain percent, depending on the stat. More powerful stats scale slower.","Crafting Dust Text 1":"Crafting Dust is a resource type that you can use to Craft Items and perform Experiments. Refer to Items tab for information on those.","Crafting Dust Text 2":"These are the four Crafting Dust types available:","Crafting Dust Text 3":"Your main source of Crafting Dust are the Dust Jars, located on the main game screen. They have a maximum amount they can hold, so make sure you on check them regularly. The Jars _do_ accumulate while being offline, as well.\n\nDust can also be obtained by completing Trials, catching Bats and can be purchased in Market.\n\nThere is a softcap on the amount of Dust you currently possess in total. It starts at 1e5 and Legacy bonuses can increase it. Once the sum of Dust of specific color you possess and have in jar is higher than this cap, the rate of accumulation starts to gradually get reduced.","Enchanting Text 1":"While accessing the Inventory and after reaching a certain Paragon level you can Enchant your Items by pressing this button next to them.","Enchanting Text 2":"To Enchant an item it must first be of either Legendary or Unique quality. Additionally, you require a certain amount of Enchanting Dust to perform the Enchantment on it.","Enchanting Text 3":"Enchanting an Item makes it grant an additional bonus, which is fixed for every Item. You can perform Enchantments up to a certain cap, which can be expanded with Memories after performing a Realm switch.\n\nWhile a single Enchantment isn't that powerful, performing multiple ones can be incredibly powerful. Each one requires exponentially more Dust to perform, but the bonus granted is multiplicative with itself.\n\nIf you have second thoughts, you can Disenchant an item to get back all the Enchanting Dust invested. This can be done by pressing this button.","Enchanting Text 4":"To Disenchant an item, you need a Nullifier, which can be obtained from various sources.","Realms Text 1":"Realms are a feature you unlock deep in the game. Access the feature by going to this tab on the Scroll, and the Upgrades by pressing this button at the bottom right of the screen.","Realms Text 2":"Realms are not unlike Exiling, but much more drastic. In addition to forfeiting your curent run, you forfeit your Mysteries, Catalyst levels and shards, Enchanting dust and Enchantment levels, Paragon levels, Gods levels and Trials done.\n\nYou retain:\n- Challenges done\n- Achievements\n- Legacy levels\n- Classes and Pets unlocks done\n- Renown levels\n- Relics, Sands, Nullifiers and Crafting Dust.\n- Memetic Splinters, both available and invested into Memetics.\n- Familiars, their Ranks and Levels.\n\nAll of your Enchanting Dust, Catalyst levels/shards and Mysteries are converted into Memories at a certain rate.","Realms Text 3":"Memories are a late-game currency that is used to purchase permanent upgrades that persists through both Exiles and Realm.\n\nMemory upgrades include two categories - Heritages and Imprints. While Heritages tend to increase your power or resource-gain overall, Imprints mostly include things that either boost the Realm switch itself or transfer some power onto the new Realm. A general recommendation is to grab some Crafting Dust from Heritage tab asap.\n\nChallenges become unlocked and auto-completed as you complete their initial requirements.\n\nPossessing multiple achievements, Legacy levels and Challenges done increases your initial progression speed dramatically, so returning to the point of previous switch will be much faster than it took initially - still, it can take quite a while.","Realms Text 4":"This is the Realms Map. Initially, you're in the Prime Realms, and several of your first Realms will still be Prime. After you possess enough Memories, you can also start exploring the Quasi-Realms. These special Realms each have their own set of rules and play quite differently to the Prime Realm. You cannot earn Memories from Quasi-Realms, but you can earn Delusions, instead.","Realms Text 5":"Delusions are a special currency that can be spent on Paramnesic upgrades. Each Quasi-Realm type grants you a specific amount of Delusions, based on your highest amount of Mana earned in it, like a highscore system. This means that earning more Delusions from a specific Quasi-Realm requires you to reach higher maximum Mana than before, after which you'll be granted the difference between your previous and current highest.\n\nSome of the Quasi-Realms also award an Enhancement upon gaining 5 total Delusions from it. Enhancements replace some of your basic Spells with new, improved versions that can have new scalings or mechanics - always strictly better than before.","Pantheon Text 1":"Pantheon is a late-game feature that allows you to accumulate a certain amount of power over time. It features a set of Gods you can worship. \n\nFor that purpose, you possess a number of Acolytes, which you can assign to specific Gods to pray to them, while free Acolytes will instead grant progress towards gaining Boons (Offerings, Scriptures, Tenets). Boons gain this speed nonlinearly, and rarer Boons require more Acolytes available at once. You can invest these Boons into specific Gods to various effects: Offering double XP gained for an hour, Scriptures boost XP gained and Tenets increase either the first or the second Ability Power, with both Scriptures and Tenets lasting until Realming. Excess Offerings can also be used for Rites (instantly gaining 30 minutes of Gods XP for each God with an Acolyte, costing 1 Offering per active God).\n\nCertain features also allow them to bestow more than one Bonus, to receive experience faster, to gain more Acolytes or even open new Gods.\n\nYou receive the bonus even if a certain God is not selected currently.\n\nTo access the Pantheon, reach Paragon 30 and press this button:","Pantheon Text 2":"Even later in the game, you will unlock Minor Gods. While also offering a different set of bonuses, their main feature is their specific way of leveling - instead of growing naturally, Minor Gods leech experience off of usual Gods, each having their own set of Gods to leech from.","Memetics Text 1":"Memetics is a late-game feature that you gain access to at e650, e750, e850, e950 and e1500 Mysteries for Void Memetics, Catalyst Memetics, Source Memetics, Spell Memetics and Pantheon Memetics, respectively.\n\nPress this button to open the Memetics menu:","Memetics Text 2":"Memetics make use of Memetic Splinters to power up. These can be gained by Experimenting and completing Key-locked Expeditions.\n\nFor more info on Catalyst Memetics, refer to Catalysts page.\n\nVoid Memetics augment either Void Mana Profits and Void Mana Per Entity, or Liquid Shadow Per Clot, and function somewhat similar to Void Entity Traps. To switch to them - click the \"echoes\" button in the Memetics screen. Collecting Echoes grants each corresponding Echo Trap xp - which, in turn, provides bonuses based on its current Level. Two remaining Traps provide bonuses to Echo spawn rate and xp per echo.\n\nEach Trap is limited by a Max Level you can level up it to, and Memetic Splinters need to be expended to increase it. Invested Max Level is permanent across Realms, while XP gained is lost upon starting a new Realm.\n\nOnce an Echo Trap is at max level, collecting further Echoes will instead increase total efficiency of all traps in a linear manner, with the exception of Spawn Speed trap.\n\nSource Memetics is a mechanic that changes how the Sources' per-25 multipliers work. Instead of providing profit, you can allow those bonuses to provide various bonuses, and augment a single Source to cost way less, this providing way more profit - usually a Source a Class benefits most from is chosen for this purpose.\n\nInstead of investing Splinters directly, instead you convert those into Memetic Ingots, which in turn are used for inividual Source Memetics. Unlike other Splinter mechanics, Ingots can be reset, but only on Exile.\nIngots invested are also un-assigned upon switching Realms, preserving their amount but allowing you to re-distribute those on reaching e850 again.\n\nSpell Memetics allow you to augment your Spells and Spell Shard Pool. Spell Memetics utilize Memetic Ink, similar to Memetic Ingots usage by Source Memetics. Investing Ink into a Spell increases it's personal Efficiency and, at certain levels, grants additional bonuses that depend on the Spell's type. Additionally, every Level makes the Spell cost more. You can always lower the current actual level without reducing the maximum level if you're running into cost issues.\n\nInvesting Ink also slowly raises your Spell Memetic Mastery level, which grants a variety of passive bonuses.\n\nMemetic Ink can also be invested into the Shard Pool to increase its capacity. This is useful to keep up with the Spell costs increasing along with their Level.\n\nPantheon Memetics allow you to either assign a new bonus to a God, with one special modifier replacing it completely. These come at a price of slower experience gain. Similar to previous two, instead of investing Splinters directly you buy Memetic Wax and buy levels of those bonuses, instead.","Familiars Text 1":"These are Familiars:","Familiars Text 2":"Familiars are a set of tiny helpful creatures with their own unique bonuses. Each Familiar has a preset rarity, an upgradeable Rank and a slowly growing Level. Gaining Familiars is done through opening Reliquaries, which can be gained through Corruptions, Trials and events.\n\nGaining copies grants currency that can be used to raise a Familiar's Rank. Each Familiar has a Type. Total Ranks of a Type affect the ability power of that Type. Rank also affects the leveling speed of this Familiar. Familiars selected can be reset upon Exile, and you can have up to three of them simultaneously based on you total Familiar Level. The Level of a Familiar also defines the strength of its ability and availability of Perks. The total Rank of all Familiars also slightly increases the average Rank of the Familiars you gain from Reliquaries.\n\nThe main ability of a Familiars also works when it is not selected, but at a significantly reduced power.","Market Text 1":"Market is the place to spend your Relics. Do note that not all of the game mechanics support this.
